司法院大法官會台字第12 6 4號鑑定意見發言要點
李念祖
一 、關於破除性別歧視 （ 憲法增修條文第10條第 6 項）

> 釋 字第748號解釋出現後必須重新檢視刑法的配偶定義與釋
字 第 554號 解 釋 。
> 婚姻制度的演變，已從過去不平等的身分性別關係轉變為現
代性別平等的契約關係。
> 配偶的不通姦義務， 是契約當事人的原生義務， 不是親族間
的出嫁從夫義務； 第三人的不相姦義務， 則是婚姻登記後的
法律衍生義務， 兩者之性質不同。民法未能明示兩項義務，
只能透過其他條文意會， 不無闕憾。
> 用刑法懲處通姦與相姦行為， 是傳統禮教人倫秩序， 強制建
立男尊女卑身分關係，出禮入刑的思想遺留； 刑法雖採看似
性別中立的法條文字， 仍對男尊女卑的社會刻板意識欠缺免
疫 力 。配偶可選擇處罰同性相姦者而宥恕異性配偶的告訴乃
論制度(刑法第2 3 9條以及刑訴第2 3 9條但書，有使刑罰公權
力成為配偶復仇的工具， 並且繼續復刻並延長鞏固男尊女卑、
不平等的身分性別關係之虞。
• 印度 Dipak Misra 大法官在 2018 年 Joseph Shine V . Union of
India — 案判決開篇之處即云：「 任何制度歧視女性， 或侵
犯女性尊嚴、 公允與平等原則者， 都足以在憲法上引起憤
慨 ； 以致於逝者如斯而不舍晝夜， 即使過去數十年間曾有
任何相關法令條文得到沈默的支持背書， 隨著日趨成熟的


===== [PDF_PAGE_0002] =====

蕙法觀念與思想的進步， 應該已經到了為之書寫輓歌的時
候 。 要求女性按照男性的或社會期待的方式思考， 足以摧
毀女性的核心自我認同， 堪 稱 可 惡 ； 宣布丈夫不是主人的
時 刻 ，已當其時1。」 （Any system treating a woman with indignity ,
inequity and inequality or discrimination invites the wrath of the
Constitution . Any provision that might have , few decades back , gothe
stamp of serene aproval may have to met its epitaph witheflux of
time and growing constitutional precepts and progresive perception . A
woman canot be asked to think as a man or as how the society desires .
Such a thought is abominable , for it slaughters her core identity . And , it
is time to say that a husband is nothe master .)
參 反 過 來 看 ， 如果主張相關條文是在保障弱勢女性， 印度最
高法院引用南韓憲法院宣告通姦罪違憲之判決論述如
下 ：「 過去南韓確實將通姦罪看成是用來保障弱勢女性的
規 定 。 以為女性在社會與經濟上處於弱勢， 違法通姦的主
要是男性。因 此 ， 通姦罪成為在心理上嚇阻男性的立法，
同時也幫助女性配偶獲得金錢賠償， 或是以分配財產做為
撤銷告訴的條件。 然 而 ， 社會的變遷稀釋了通姦犯罪刑事
立法的正當性。 最緊要的是女性的收入及經濟能力因社會

===== [PDF_PAGE_0003] =====

與經濟活動的能量而進步， 女性是經濟上的弱勢假設， 不
再普遍適用於已婚的配偶 。 2」 （The Court also analyzed the
argumenthat adultery provisions protected women : “It is true thathexistence of adultery crimes in the past Korean society served to protect
women . Women were socialy and economicaly underprivileged , and
acts of adultery were mainly comited by men . Therefore , thexistence of an adultery crime acted as psychological deterence for
men , and , furthermore , enabled female spouses to receive payment of
compensation for grief or divided asets from the male spouse on the
condition of canceling the adultery acusation . However , the changes
of our society diluted the justification of criminal punishment of
adultery . Above al , as women’s earning power and economicapabilities have improved with more active social and economic
activities , the premise that women are theconomicaly disadvantagedoes not aply to al maried couples . )
> 歷年相關刑事資料統計數字足以證明， 傳統上男尊女卑之社
會刻板意識仍然存在而且緣附於刑法第2 3 9及刑訴2 3 9但書
作 用 ， 延續了通姦關係中男性受到宥恕而女性不受宥恕的優
越 地 位 ； 婚姻的性別平等契約關係， 也因之回到了不平等的
身分性別關係。
> 法官紛基於保障性別平等的理由聲請釋憲， 堪可證明業已
普遍體認自身負有消除性別歧視， 促進性別平等的憲法義務
2 同前註。


===== [PDF_PAGE_0004] =====

二 、關於比例原則
> 較小之侵害手段， 至 少 有 二 ， 一是於 民法中明確載入 ， 有配
> 二是 只罰配偶， 以避免性別歧視 。 但大法官必須補充釋748

與 職 責 （ 憲法增修條文第10條 第 6 項 ） 。 大院為憲法機關，
負有相同的憲法義務。 釋 字第 3 72號即為使用此條項之一項
前 例 。
> 本案系爭法律限制基本人權 （ 包括憲法第22條所保障者， 無
論以隱私權， 或如釋字第554號以性自主自由稱之皆無不可） ，
涉及實際性別歧視 （ 釋 字第 6 號 ）與限制人身自由的刑罰
規 定 ， 應依比例原則施以嚴格審查。
> 系爭規定表面上在防止通姦以維繫婚姻， 卻在實質配合配偶
復仇並延續也助長男尊女卑之社會刻板意識， 即不能認為具
有特別重要之公共利益。
> 刑罰追訴能否直接而實質地達到預防通姦與維繫婚姻的實際
效 用 ， 不能徒憑假設。沒有實證資料顯示獲得宥恕配偶之再
犯率及其婚姻之維持率如何，即無從證明其手段必要性果然
存 在 。
偶者不得通姦， 以及第三人不得與他人之配偶為性行為兩項
義 務 ， 並提高賠償額度與慰撫金， 以替代刑罰， 也可避免除
罪化引起通姦具有社會相當性的誤會，同時消除國庫與民事
原告自被告之財產求償發生任何競爭關係。
解 釋 ， 說明配偶的定義是否不因性傾向而有差異。
> 以上二者均指向系爭規定不符限制妥當性而屬違憲立法的結


===== [PDF_PAGE_0005] =====

論 。
> 至於主張民事賠償是給予富人以金錢換取不法的說法， 至少
有兩個錯誤。
1 . 此一論據如果成立， 民法賠償責任制度， 豈不盡在獎勵富
人為侵權行為而悉應改以刑罰施之？民事法院原得斟酌
富人被告之財力而判給與其財力相當之賠償， 此則與刑罰
秩序無涉。問題不只在於損害賠償並不等於用金錢換取違
法 行 為 ， 也在違約的公平法律救濟， 並不依賴施以刑罰嚇
阻 補 充 。
2 . 在婚姻私事上也迷信刑罰萬能而不重民事救濟， 正是國家
秩序無遠弗屆，出禮入刑， 刑罰直入家門以貫徹人倫秩序
的德治傳統思想路徑。而通姦成罪， 以刑替民甚或以刑逼
民 ， 是民事救濟欠發達的舊時法律文化特徵， 也是婚姻制
度 ， 從身分轉變到契約迄今猶是困難重， 無法擺脫禮教
社會中男性配偶的優越地位， 也難以破除社會性別刻板意
識的重大障礙。

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA

Dipak Misra， CJI (For himself and A . M . Khanwilkar , J . )
The beauty of the Indian Constitution is that it includes ' I ‘
" you ’ and We ’ . Such a magnificent , compasionate and
monumental document embodies emphatic inclusivenes whichas ben further nurtured by judicial sensitivity when it has
developed the concept of golden triangle ofundamental rights . If
we have to aply the parameters of a fundamental right , it is an
expresion of judicial sensibility which further enhances the
beauty of the Constitution as conceived of . In such a situation ,
thesentiality of the rights of women gets the real requisite
space in the living rom of individual dignity rather than the

1


===== [PDF_PAGE_0007] =====

space in anexe to the main building . That is the
manifestation of concerned sensitivity . Individual dignity has a
sanctified realm in a civilized society . The civility of a civilization
earns warmth and respect when it respects more the
individuality of a woman . The said concept gets a further acent
when a woman is treated withe real spirit of equality with a
man . Any system treating a woman with indignity , inequity and
inequality or discrimination invites the wrath of the Constitution .
Any provision that might have , few decades back , gothe stamp
of serene aproval may have to met its epitaph witheflux of
time and growing constitutional precepts and progresive
perception . A woman canot be asked to think as a man or as
how the society desires . Such a thought is abominable , for it
slaughters her core identity . And , it is time to say that a
husband is nothe master . Equality is the governing parameter .
Al historical perceptionshould evaporate and their obituaries
be writen . It is advisable to rember what John Stuart Mil had
observed : -
“The legal subordination of one sex to another -
is wrong in itself , and now one of the chief
hindrances to human improvement ; and that it
oughto be replaced by a system of perfect

equality , admiting no power and privilege on
the one side , nor disability on the other . ” 1
We are comencing withe aforesaid prefatory note as we
are adverting to the constitutional validity of Section 497 of the
Indian Penal Code ( IPC ) and Section 198 of the Code of Criminal
Procedure ( CrPC ) .
2 . Athis juncture , it is necesary to state thathough there is
necesity of certainty of law , yet withe societal changes and
more so , when the rights arexpanded by the Court in respect of
certain aspects having regard to the reflective perception of the
organic and living Constitution , it is not aposite to have an
inflexible stand on the foundation thathe concept of certainty of
law should be alowed to prevail and govern . The progresion in
law and the perceptual shift compels the presento have a
penetrating lok to the past .
3. When we say so , we may not be understod that precedents
are noto be treated asuch and that in thexcuse of perceptual
shift , the binding nature of precedent should not be alowed to
retain itstatus or alowed to be diluted . When a constitutional
court facesuch a chalenge , namely , to be detained by a
precedent or to grow out of the same because of the normative

6. Athistage , one aspect neds to be noted . Athe time of
initial hearing before the thre-Judge Bench , the decision in
Yusuf Abdul Aziz ( supra ) was cited and the cited Law Report
reflected thathe judgment was delivered by four learned Judges
and later on , it was noticed , as is reflectible from the Supreme
Court Reports , thathe decision was rendered by a Constitution
Bench comprising ofive Judges of this Court .
7 . The said factual discovery wil not detain us any further . In
Yusuf Abdul Aziz ( supra ) , the Court was dealing withe
controversy that had traveled to this Court while dealing with a
diferent fact situation . In the said case , the question arose
whether Section 497 contravened Articles 14 and 15 of the
Constitution of India . In the said case , the apelant was being
prosecuted for adultery under Section 497 IPC . Ason as the
complaint was filed , the husband aplied to the High Court of
Bombay to determine the constitutional question under Article
228 of the Constitution . The Constitution Bench refering to
Section 497 held thus : -
“ 3. Under Section 497 the ofence of
adultery can only be comited by a man
but in the absence of any provision to the
contrary the woman would be punishable as
an abetor . The last sentence in Section 497
prohibits this . It runs —

7


===== [PDF_PAGE_0013] =====

“In such case the wife shal not be
punishable as an abetor . ” It isaid thathis ofends Articles 14 and 15 .
The portion of Article 15 on which the
apelant relies is this :
“The State shal not discriminate against
any citizen on grounds only of . sex . ”
But what he overloks is that isubjecto clause ( 3) which runs —
“Nothing in this article shal preventhe
State fromaking any special provision for
women . ”
The provision complained of is a special
provision and it is made for women ,
therefore it isaved by clause ( 3).
4 . It was argued that clause ( 3) should be
confined to provisions which are beneficial
to women and canot be used to give them
a licence to comit and abet crimes . We are
unable to read any such restriction into the
clause ; nor are we able to agre that a
provision which prohibits punishment is
tantamounto a licence to comithe
ofence of which punishment has ben
prohibited . 5
5 . Article 14 is general and must be read
withe other provisions which set outhe
ambit ofundamental rights . Sex is a sound
clasification and although there can be no
discrimination in general on that ground ,
the Constitution itself provides for special
provisions in the case of women and
children . The two articles read together
validate the impugned clause in Section 4 9 7
of the Indian Penal Code .

1 3. We have noticed a judgment of the Division Bench of the

“ Alaws in force in the teritory of India
imediately before the comencement of this

Therefore, before a law in force can be declared to

2

Fundamental Rights, and the fundamental right

remain on the statute bok. The whole scheme of
Art. 1 3 is to make laws, which are inconsistent with
Part I, void, not only if they were in force before the
comencement of the Constitution, but also if they
werenacted after the Constitution came into force.
Mr. Patel relies on the various provisos to Art. 19

generaly with any special provision for women and

made in future. Buthexception made to Art. 1 5( 1 )
by Art. 1 5(3) is an exception which aplies both to
existing laws and to laws which the State may make

taken on the ground that provisions penalising adultery are discriminatory against
wom en either patently on the face of the law or in their implem entation. Reform
towards achieving a m oregalitarian society in practice has also ben driven by
active m easures taken by the United Nations and other international human
rights organizations, w here it has ben em phasized that even sem ingly gender-
neutral provisions criminalising adultery cast an unequal burden on w om en:63
“ Given continuediscrimination and inequalities faced by
women, including inferioroles atributed to them by
patriarchal and traditional atitudes, and power imbalances in
theirelations with men, the mere fact of maintaing adultery
as a criminal ofence, even when it aplies to both women
and men, means in practice that women mainly wil continue
to facextreme vulnerabilities, and violation of their human
rights to dignity, privacy and equality.”
The abolishing of adultery has ben brought about in equal m easure by
legislatures and courts. W hen decisions have ben handedown by the judiciary
acros the world, it has led to the creation of a rich body of transnational
jurisprudence. Thisection wil focus on a few select com parative decisions
em anating from the courts of those countries w here the provision criminalizing
adultery has ben struck down through judicial action. The decisions of these

Next, the Court analysed the apropriatenes and efectivenes of criminal

PART D
The decisions of the USuprem e Court bearing on the isue of privacy have
ben analysed in an incisive article, titled “For Beter or for W orse: Adultery,
Crim e and The Constitution”78, by Martin Siegel. He presents thre ways in which
adultery implicates the righto privacy. The first is that adultery must be viewed
as a constitutionaly protected marital choice. Second, that certain adulterous
relationships are protected by the fredom of asociation and finaly, that adultery
constitutes an action which is protected by sexual privacy.79 A brief study is also
undertaken on w hether action penalizing adultery constitutes a legitim ate interest
of the State.
The first privacy interest in adultery is the righto marital choice. The U.S.
Suprem e Court has upheld the values of ‘fundam entaliberty', ‘fredom of
choice’ and ‘the ‘righto privacy’ in m ariage. W ithis jurisprudence, the author
argues, it would be strange if a decision to comit adultery is not a treated as a
m ater of m ariage and fam ily life as expresed in C leveland B o ard8 0 , ‘an act
ocuring in m ariage’， as held in G risw o ld 8 1 or a ‘m ater of m ariage and family
life’ as elucidated in C arey.82

Siegel cites Laurence Tribe, on acepting the ‘unconventional variants' that also

The m ere facthat adultery is considered unconventional in society does not

encom pases the fredom of making an ‘unpopular' choice. This was articulated
by Justice Blackmun in his disent in H ard w ick84:
“ A necesary corolary of giving individuals fredom to chose

diferent individuals wil make diferent choices.” 8 5

important, although "loveles encounters are sometimes

Next, Siegel exam ines the plausible protection of adultery through the lens of the
fredom of expresion. Since the act of engaging in sexual activity can be

held that First Am endm ent rights are not limited to m erely verbal expresion but

“ The righto privacy enables an individual to exercise his or

6 0 Recognition of sexual autonom y as inhering in each individual and of thelem ents of privacy andignity have a bearing on the role of the state in
regulating the conditions and consequences of marital relationships. There is a
fundam ental reason which militates against criminalization of adultery. Its
genesis lies in the facthat criminalizing an act is not a valid constitutional
response to a sexual relationship outside the fold of m ariage. Adultery in the
course of a subsisting marital relationship m ay, and very often does question the
com itm ent of the spouse to the relationship. In m any cases, a sexual
relationship of one of the spouses outside of the m ariage m ay lead to thend of
the marital relationship. But in other cases, such a relationship m ay not be the
cause buthe consequence of a pre-existing disruption of the marital tie. Al toften, spouses who have drifted apart irevocably m ay be com peled foreasons
personal to them to continue withe vener of a m ariage whichas ended for
al intents and purposes. The interminably long delay of the law in the resolution
of m atrimonial conflicts is an aspect which canot be ignored. The realities of
hum an existence are to com plex to place them in closed categories of right and
wrong and to subject al that is considered wrong withe sanctions of penalaw.
Just as al conduct which is not criminal m ay not necesarily bethicaly just, al
conduct which is inapropriate does not justify being elevated to a criminal
wrongdoing.

W hen the statenacts and enforcesuch legislation, it doeson the postulate
that m ariage as a social institution has a significant bearing on the social fabric.
But in doing so, the state is equaly governed by the norms of a liberal
Constitution which em phasise dignity, equality and liberty as its cardinal values.
The legitim ate aim s of the state may, it must be recognized, extend to imposing

deleterious to hum an dignity. In criminalizing certain types of wrongdoing against
wom en, the state intervenes to protecthe fundam ental rights of every wom an to
live with dignity. Consequently, it is importanto underscore thathis judgm ent

fundam ental rights of wom en from being tram pled upon in unequal societal

H Towards transformative justice

PART H
disregards her sexuality. The sexuality of a wom an is part of her inviolable core.
Neither the state nor the institution of m ariage can disparage it. By reducing the
wom an to the status of a victim and ignoring her neds, the provision penalizing
adultery disregardsomething which is basic to human identity. Sexuality is a

intrinsic to autonomy. It is in this view of the m ater that w e have concluded that
Section 497 is violative of the fundam ental rights to equality and liberty as
inded, the righto pursue a m eaningfulife within the fold of Articles 1 4 and 2 1 .
62 The halm ark of a truly transform ative Constitution is that it promotes and
engendersocietal change. To consider a fre citizen as the property of another
is anathem a to the ideal of dignity. Section 497 denies the individual identity
of a maried w om an, based on age-old societal stereotypes whicharacterised
wom en as the property of their spouse. It is the duty of this Courto break these
stereotypes and promote a society which regards wom en as equal citizens in al
spheres of life- irespective of w hether these spheres m ay be regarded as ‘public’
or ‘private’.

constitutional values of equality, dignity and autonomy.

Adultery undoubtedly is a moral w r o n g qua the spouse

a n d the family. The isue is whethere is a suficient
element of wrongfulnes to society in general, in order to
bring it within the ambit of criminalaw?
T helement of publicensure, visiting the delinquent
with penal consequences, a n d overiding individual
rights, would be justified only w h e n the society is directly
impacted by such conduct. In fact, a m u c h stronger
justification is required where a n ofence is punishable
with imprisonment.
T he State m u s t folow the minimalist aproach in the
criminalization ofences, keping in view the respect for
the a u t o n o m y of the individual to m a k e his/her personal
choices.
